Internal convolution via FFT:
def fast_convolve(a):
    n=a.size; N=1
    while N < 2*n-1: N*=2
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    return np.fft.irfft(fa*fa, N)[:2*n-1]
For power of two maybe use scipy? np.fft enough.

Then optimizing block values: We can use continuous optimization methods that call fast_convolve many times.

Alternative to LP: Since m moderate, we can use non-linear optimization of block values directly, avoiding LP overhead. But LP direction has good properties.

Let's define internal fast score for block sequence:
- Given actual a length n sum=1, compute c via fft (N next power of two >=2n-1), M=max(c[:2n-1]), score=2*n*M. Since sum=1. This is scale invariant; if a normalized different, internal score =2nM/sum^2. We can use. For final, use evaluate_sequence.
- To avoid FFT errors/overflow, c normalized ~1/n ~1e-4; exp not needed.

Block LP iteration function:
def block_lp_step(a, m, lengths, rng, use_lb=True):
    n = len(a)
    # compute convolution max
    c = fft_convolve(a)
    M = max(c)
    if M <=0: return a, M, False
    # Build S maybe using grouped prefix for current a and lengths
    S = build_S(a, lengths) # shape (2n-1,m)
    # Solve LP: max sum(g) s.t. S g <= M*(1+1e-9), g>=0
    c_obj = -np.ones(m)
    b = np.full(2n-1, M)
    # maybe remove rows where S row all zeros (none)
    res = linprog(c_obj, A_ub=S, b_ub=b, bounds=[(0,None)]*m, method='highs')
    if res.status not in (0,? ) or not finite: fallback direction = random positive
    g = res.x
    # If g sum small, try adding small current a direction
    g_sum = g.sum()
    if not finite or g_sum <= 1e-12: g = current block values (extract)
    # Map to array and normalize to sum 1
    g_arr = np.repeat(g, lengths)
    total = g_arr.sum()
    if total<=0: fail
    g_arr = g_arr/total
    # Try blend alphas
    best = a, best_score current
    for alpha in [0.5,0.25,0.1,0.05,0.9,0.95?]:
       cand = (1-alpha)*a + alpha*g_arr
       cand = np.clip(cand,0,None); normalize
       score = fast_score(cand)
       if score < best_score - 1e-12: best
    return best a, score, improved

Let's think convergence of block LP. The LP objective maximize sum(g) given current a. If a is block-constant and we restrict g block-constant, the mapping a <- blend(a, normalize(g)) may reach a fixed point where a is proportional to g? At fixed point, LP solution g is a scalar multiple of a. Then a is optimal for block family? There is a relation similar to iterative proportional fitting: a_k ∝ Σ_t λ_t S[t,k]. def normalize_nonneg(x, target_sum=1.0):
    x = np.asarray(x,float); x[x<0]=0; s=x.sum(); if s<=1e-12: return None; return x*(target_sum/s)

Fast convolution:
def fast_full_convolution(x):
    n=x.size; if n==0 return empty; L=2*n-1; N=1; while N<L: N*=2; fx=np.fft.rfft(x,N); out=np.fft.irfft(fx*fx,N)[:L]; out=np.maximum(out,0)? FFT small negative; clip 0.
Return out.

Potential FFT phase: For real convolution, irfft of |? We need square of FFT, not power (no conjugate). `fx*fx` (not fx*fx.conj()) gives DFT of convolution x*x (linear, not circular) because both sequences same and linear zero-padded. Yes, product not conjugate. If we used power, that would be autocorrelation reversed. We need convolution with same sequence: DFT(x) * DFT(x). Correct.

Test small: x=[1,2], N=4, rfft [3,0?], square, irfft should [1,4,4]? Actually conv [1,4,4]. Using fx*fx yes. Using |fx|^2 would [1,4,4]? For real two? Might same? For [1,2,3], product vs power differ. We need product.

Internal score:
def fast_score(x):
    x = np.asarray(x); s=x.sum(); if s <=0: return inf
    # normalize not necessary but for stable
    y=x/s
    c=fast_full_convolution(y)
    m=c.max()
    return 2*y.size*m  # since sum=1
But if x not normalized, score =2n*m/(s^2). Could compute directly to avoid scaling? Scaling may cause small values; okay.

For sequence length maybe 6962, c values ~1e-4, max. Score ~1.5.

LP solve helper:
def solve_direction_lp(S, M, m):
    c_obj = -np.ones(m)
    b = np.full(S.shape[0], M, dtype=np.float64)
    # remove constraints with all zero? find rows where S.sum(axis=1)>0
    rows = np.where(np.any(S > 1e-300, axis=1))[0]
    if len(rows) < S.shape[0]: S=S[rows]; b=b[rows]
    # optional: if constraints too many and time, random subset? Keep all.
    try:
       res=linprog(c_obj, A_ub=S, b_ub=b, bounds=[(0,None)]*m, method='highs', options={'time_limit': 20.0, 'presolve': True})
       if res.status == 0: return res.x, True
    except: pass
    # fallback direction via dual? return None

Potential LP objective unbounded? HiGHS status 3? We'll fallback. `bounds=[(0,None)]*m` top-level okay (list created in helper). No lambda.

Fallback direction: Use topK weights and compute g? For subgradient, we want a direction to blend. We can set g = current block values (v) plus negative gradient? Actually we need a nonnegative direction h (array) to blend. We can compute gradient grad = 2 S^T y_top, then h = np.maximum(v - step*grad,0). But we don't have step. Simpler fallback: use a random perturbation of v.

Block LP optimize function:
def optimize_block(n, m, lengths, v0, iters, deadline, rng, verbose=False):
    v = v0.copy(); normalize v: total = v @ lengths; v /= total.
    a = np.repeat(v, lengths)
    score = fast_score(a)
    base_score = score
    last_improve = 0
    alpha = 0.5
    for it in range(iters):
       if time.monotonic() > deadline: break
       a = np.repeat(v, lengths) # maybe avoid rebuild if no change
       c = fast_full_convolution(a)
       M = c.max()
       S = build_S_from_array(a, lengths)
       g = solve_direction_lp(S, M, m, time_limit=...)
       if g is None:
           # subgradient top K
           K = max(1, len(c)//200)
           idx = c.argsort()[-K:]
           w = np.zeros(len(c)); w[idx]=1.0/K
           grad = 2.0 * (S.T @ w)
           # choose step: lr = 1.0 / (np.linalg.norm(grad, ord=inf)+1e-12)
           h = v - lr*grad
           h = np.maximum(h,0)
           if h@lengths <=0: h=v*rng.normal(1,0.1)
           ha = np.repeat(h/ (h@lengths), lengths)
       else:
           ha = np.repeat(g, lengths); total = ha.sum(); ha=ha/total
       # line search alphas
       best_cand = a; best_sc = score
       for alpha in [0.5,0.25,0.1,0.05,0.75,0.9]:
          cand = (1-alpha)*a + alpha*ha
          cand = normalize_nonneg(cand)
          if cand is None: continue
          sc = fast_score(cand)
          if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
             best_cand=cand; best_sc=sc
       if best_sc < score - 1e-12:
          v = block_values(best_cand, lengths) # average per block maybe not constant after blend? Blend of two block constant remains block constant. But subgradient h block constant; yes. Use mean to avoid numerical.
          v = v / (v@lengths)
          score = best_sc
          # maybe adjust alpha? keep 0.5
       else:
          # reduce alpha? Try smaller; if still no, break
          if alpha <=0.02: break
       optional print every 20 improvements.
    return a, score

But note: after blend, if `ha` block constant and `a` block constant, candidate block constant. Compute v as mean of each block to enforce. `block_values(a, lengths)` returns [a[start:end].mean()...] then normalize.

But the LP objective sum over g_k (not weighted by length) means it biases toward blocks with longer lengths? Original full LP objective sum over n positions. If we restrict to block-constant g per-position value g_k, the sum over positions is Σ g_k length_k, not Σ g_k. Our LP should maximize Σ g_k length_k to mimic original. Good catch. In block LP, objective should be maximize Σ_k length_k * g_k. Because full sequence g has n variables, sum(g)=Σ_k length_k g_k. Constraints in terms of per-position values. So c_obj = -lengths (not -ones). Bounds on g_k per position. This is important. Also when normalizing direction, sum_array = lengths@g. Good.

So LP: c = -lengths.astype(float). Max lengths@g. Feasible g=a (per-position) has sum lengths@a =1. Good.

Similarly, if blocks have different lengths, subgradient update variable v is per-position value, objective gradient as above.

Let's analyze LP direction more. Suppose current a has max at some t. The LP max sum(g) s.t. a*g ≤ M. This is the polar of the convex set {a*g ≤ M}. If a is fixed, the optimal g is a "dual" distribution. Blending a with g may reduce max. This is nonconvex but SOTA says works.

We can also run the LP with `M = max(a*a) * (1 + decay)` where decay decreases? If M fixed to current max, direction g is feasible. If we relax M (increase), g may be larger in high-load directions, not helpful. Use current M.

Maybe we need to use `f` in constraints as current `a`, but if a is normalized sum=1, M~1e-4. LP coefficients S~ lengths? Let's ensure scaling. a per position ~1/n=1.4e-4. S_tk sum over block length L ~ L*1/n ~ 0.14 for m=59,L=118. M ~0.7e-4. Constraint S g ≤ 7e-5 => g ≤5e-6 per-position. Current a ~1.4e-4, so direction g is about 3x smaller? But feasible a*g = c ≤M: S@a? S coefficients ~0.14, a~1.4e-4 => product ~2e-5 per block? Sum over blocks m~59 =>1.2e-3? Wait M~0.75/n=1.1e-4. Maybe scale. Anyway g scale small; normalizing direction okay. LP objective lengths@g maximizes total array sum (scale-free? Adding scale to g increases objective until constraints bind). The feasible set includes g=0 and g=a? Check g=a per-position: S @ a_block =? For block constant, (a*g)_t = c_t ≤M. So g=a feasible. Objective lengths@a =1. LP optimum objective ≥1. So g values not too small. Good.

Now, full local search random perturbations. We need avoid making score worse. Starting from optimized block sequence, random noise likely bad. But we can use very small perturbations and accept improvements. Implementation:
- For a number of iterations until time, take best a, create candidate with:
   * With prob 0.5: add Gaussian noise with sigma = 0.01 * v (per position), clip≥0, normalize.
   * With prob 0.3: choose random block (or segment) length 10-500, multiply by exp(N(0,0.05)) or shift values, normalize.
   * With prob 0.2: move mass between two random positions: choose i,j, delta = small * a[i], a[i]-=delta,a[j]+=delta.
- Evaluate fast_score; if improves, accept and maybe restart with smaller/larger noise.
This may slowly improve finite effects.

But random search in 6962-dimensional space with complex objective likely inefficient. Better use gradient descent with topK subgradient.

Full gradient descent algorithm:
- Start from best a (block or whatever), normalized sum=1.
- Use topK average objective (K maybe 5,20,50) to get a smooth subgradient.
- Compute c=fft conv; select top K indices; weights y = 1/K on them.
- Compute grad = 2 * (convolve(y_rev, a)[n-2:2n-1][::-1]) as derived. Let's implement a robust function `fast_gradient_from_weights(y, a, N)`:
   n=a.size; L=2*n-1; Nfft = next power of two >= L + n -1? For conv(y_rev, a), length L+n-1=3n-2. Use N2 next pow2 >=3n-2.
   fa = rfft(a, N2); fy = rfft(y[::-1], N2); full = irfft(fa*fy,N2)
   grad = 2 * full[n-2 : 2*n-2+1][::-1]  # length n (indices 2n-2 down to n-2)
Check for n=1: L=1, full length? n-2=-1 bad. Handle n=1 separately. For n>=2. For n=2, indices? n=2, want full[2:4?]. 2n-2=2, n-2=0, slice full[0:3][::-1] length3? Wait for n=2, L=3, conv length L+n-1=4. We want indices 2,1? i=0 -> full[2], i=1 -> full[1]. That's 2 indices. Our slice full[n-2:2n-2+1] = full[0:3] length3, too many. Let's re-evaluate. For n=2, L=3, rev conv length 4 (indices 0..3). Formula grad[i]=full[2n-2 - i] = full[2 - i]. i=0 index2, i=1 index1. So indices from 1 to2 descending. Start=2n-2-(n-1)=n-1=1, end=2n-2=2. So slice full[start:end+1][::-1], start=n-1, end=2n-2. For n=3, start=2,end=4, correct. For n=2, start=1,end=2. So use full[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]? End exclusive 2n-1? For n=3: full[2:5] length3 good. n=2: full[1:3] indices1,2. So grad=2*full[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]. For n=1: full[0:1] maybe. Good.
Let's verify with earlier n=3: full[2:5] reversed. Yes.
So code: start=n-1; stop=2*n-1; grad=2*full[start:stop][::-1].
- Ensure grad finite; if not fallback.
- Update: We need project to sum=1. A good step: new = a - lr * grad; new = max(new,0); normalize. But if lr too large negative, normalize. We can use relative step. `lr = max_step / max(1, np.linalg.norm(grad/ a.mean(), ord=∞))`? Since grad ~2a ~2e-4, a~1e-4, ratio ~2. Step lr*grad ~lr*2e-4. Want change maybe 0.1*a ~1e-5, lr ~0.05. Try lr values [0.1,0.05,0.01,0.2].
- Use topK K schedule: start small (1) exact subgradient, but noisy; larger (100) smoother. We can try multiple K.
- Accept if score improves. If no improvement for many steps, reduce K or change lr.
- This is basically projected stochastic gradient. May help refine.

But implementing full gradient with FFT each iteration O(N log N) for N~3n (20k) very fast (~0.0005s). We can do thousands.

However, topK subgradient may push to zero/negative; normalize. Could escape local minima.

Maybe a simpler and effective update: use multiplicative Weiszfeld-like:
For weights y top, load_k = (S^T y)_k for blocks or per position load_i = Σ_t y_t a[t-i] (half gradient). Update a_i *= exp(-η load_i) or 1/(1+η load_i). Starting from block sequence, this can adjust within blocks. Since objective max constraints, positions contributing to high conv are reduced. Then normalize.
For full per-position load L[i] = Σ_t y_t a[t-i] = grad/2. We can compute via FFT. Then multiplicative update `new = a / (1 + η L)` (or exp). Project nonnegative, normalize. This might be stable and scale-free. Choose η such that changes moderate. Since L~1/n, η~n gives O(1). Try eta in [n/10, n/2, n]. For n=6962, eta_n ~ thousands.
This resembles iterative proportional fitting for minimizing weighted sum. Could be very effective.

Let's test conceptually: If current a has high c at some t, load L[i] = a[t-i] for active t. Reducing factors with high other-index values. We can implement a `multiplicative_gradient_step(a, y, eta)`:
   L = convolve(y, a) orientation? grad/2 = Σ_t y_t a[t-i]. This is same cross-correlation. Use FFT as above without factor 2. Then new = a / (1 + eta * L) (ensures nonnegative). Normalize. If eta=0 no change.

Top-level constants: none except maybe.

Helper functions:
1. `next_pow2(x)` returns int.

3. `fast_convolve(x)`
4. `fast_score(x)`
5. `evaluate_final(seq_list)`? We cannot import evaluate_sequence but can call. We'll just call in propose.
6. `build_S_for_blocks(a, lengths)`
7. `solve_block_lp(S, lengths, M, deadline, rng)`
   - maybe if time_left < 2 return None.
   - use linprog.
8. `block_values_from_array(a, lengths)`
   - start loop, mean.
9. `make_block_array(v, lengths)` -> repeat and normalize? Maybe v already normalized per sum? We'll ensure.
10. `initialize_v(m, kind, rng, lengths)`
    - returns per-position values v normalized sum with lengths =1.
    Kinds:
      'three': create x centers (np.arange(m)+0.5)/m; v = 3*np.ones(m); v[x>=1/3]=2; maybe if x<1/3. But for m not divisible, okay.
      'linear': v = 1 - 0.5*(np.arange(m)/(max(1,m-1)))
      'quad': v = 1 - 0.4*(t**2)
      'exp': v = np.exp(-0.5*t)
      'rand': v = rng.random(m)+0.1
    Normalize: total = v@lengths; v/=total; if negative clip.
11. `optimize_block_fixed(n, m, lengths, v_init, deadline, iters, alpha_list, subdeg=True)`
    - Uses global linprog.
    - Maintain v (per-position values) normalized.
    - a = np.repeat(v,lengths)
    - Current score via fast_score.
    - Keep history, if no improvement for `stuck` iterations break.
    - At each iter:
        if time_left < 5: break
        c = fast_convolve(a); M = c.max()
        if M <= 0: break
        S = build_S_for_blocks(a, lengths)
        g = None
        if time_left > 10:
           g = solve_block_lp(S, lengths, M, ...)
        if g is not None:
           dir_arr = np.repeat(g, lengths); normalize dir_arr
        else:
           # subgradient topK direction using additive
           choose K = max(1, len(c)//100) maybe; top_idx = np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]
           w = zeros; w[top_idx]=1/K
           grad = 2*(S.T @ w)
           # additive direction h = v - lr*grad; make nonneg
           lr = 1.0 / (np.max(np.abs(grad)) + 1e-12)
           h = v - lr*grad
           h = np.maximum(h,0)
           if h@lengths <=0: h = v
           dir_arr = np.repeat(h/(h@lengths), lengths)
        # line search
        improved=False
        best_sc=score; best_a=a; best_v=v
        for alpha in [0.5,0.25,0.1,0.6,0.15,0.05,0.02]:
           cand = (1-alpha)*a + alpha*dir_arr
           cand = normalize_nonneg(cand)
           if cand is None: continue
           # enforce block constant? cand block constant if both; due to floating okay.
           sc = fast_score(cand)
           if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
              best_sc=sc; best_a=cand; best_v=block_values_from_array(cand,lengths); best_v=best_v/(best_v@lengths); improved=True
        if improved:
           v=best_v; a=best_a; score=best_sc; stuck=0
           # print occasionally
        else:
           stuck +=1
           if stuck > 30: break
    - return a,score

Potential improvement: The line search tries fixed alphas; if best at alpha=0.5, we accept. Next iteration LP again. Good.
But if direction is poor, small alpha may improve; we accept. If none, stuck.

Let's refine `build_S_for_blocks` performance.
```
def build_S_for_blocks(a, lengths):
    n = a.size
    m = lengths.size
    L = 2*n -1
    t = np.arange(L, dtype=np.int64)
    prefix = np.empty(n+1); prefix[0]=0; np.cumsum(a, out=prefix[1:])
    S = np.empty((L,m), dtype=np.float64)
    start=0
    for k in range(m):
        end = start + int(lengths[k])
        low = np.maximum(0, t - (end - 1))
        high = np.minimum(n - 1, t - start)
        valid = high >= low
        vals = np.zeros(L, dtype=np.float64)
        if np.any(valid):
            vals[valid] = prefix[high[valid] + 1] - prefix[low[valid]]
        S[:,k]=vals
        start=end
    return S
```
This creates low/high/valid/vals each loop. Could optimize by preallocating low, high, valid, vals outside and reuse to reduce memory allocation. Top-level function can allocate inside loop; okay. But for speed, preallocate:
```
low = np.empty(L, dtype=np.int64); high=...; vals=...
for ...:
   np.maximum(0, t - (end-1), out=low)
   np.minimum(n-1, t - start, out=high)
   valid = high >= low
   vals.fill(0)
   vals[valid] = prefix[high[valid]+1] - prefix[low[valid]]
   S[:,k]=vals
```
Good.
Potential issue `prefix[high[valid]+1]` where high=-1 invalid excluded. valid high>=low and low>=0, so high>=0.

Can we compute S using convolution of block indicators with a via `np.convolve`? For m=64, each direct convolve with dense indicator length n would be O(n^2) too slow. Prefix good.

Now, LP solving options:
```
def solve_block_lp(S, lengths, M, time_limit_s=10.0):
    if linprog is None: return None
    c = -lengths.astype(float)
    b = np.full(S.shape[0], M, dtype=float)
    # If M <= 0 return None
    # Optional reduce rows: rows with S.sum(axis=1) <= 1e-300
    row_sum = np.sum(S, axis=1)
    rows = np.where(row_sum > 1e-300)[0]
    if rows.size == 0: return None
    if rows.size < S.shape[0]:
        S2 = S[rows, :]
        b2 = b[rows]
    else:
        S2 = S; b2 = b
    # Sometimes HiGHS may be slow with many nearly redundant constraints. Could randomly subsample 4096 rows if S rows > 8000 and time limit small.
    # But keep all for accuracy if rows <= 25000.
    try:
       options = {'time_limit': float(time_limit_s), 'presolve': True}
       res = linprog(c, A_ub=S2, b_ub=b2, bounds=[(0.0, None)] * S2.shape[1], method='highs', options=options)
       status = res.status
       if status == 0 or status == 1? HiGHS status 0 optimal, 1? scipy linprog status meanings: 0 optimum, 1 fail, 2 infeasible, 3 unbounded, 4 numerical. Actually status=0 only success. Use res.success.
       if res.success:
          x=np.asarray(res.x,float)
          if np.all(np.isfinite(x)) and x.min() >= -1e-12:
             x=np.maximum(x,0)
             return x
    except Exception:
       pass
    return None
```
Setting `time_limit` may cause status 14 (cancelled) not success; fallback. We can set time_limit = min(30, time_left/3) maybe. If too short, many failures. Maybe don't set time_limit; 14k constraints should be fast. But to avoid one LP eating entire budget, set time_limit=20.0. If fails, fallback.

Could use `method='highs'` only; no `method` parameter? SciPy linprog has method='highs'. Good.

If `linprog` unavailable, fallback.

Now, the block LP direction could be very small or all zeros due to time_limit. We check `g.sum()`. If `g` has zeros for some blocks, okay.

One nuance: The LP maximizes total sum of g (per-position values * lengths). The direction g may be feasible but not normalized. If all g zero due to infeasibility? g=0 feasible. But objective positive feasible (g=a). If `M` is slightly below max due to numerical and `S@a` exceeds M, `a` infeasible; LP may still find small/g? We should set `M = max(c) * (1 + 1e-9) + 1e-300`. Also, if using FFT clipped, c max maybe slightly under exact. To ensure a feasible, compute S0 = S (current) @ v? Actually S @ v (v per-position) gives a*a? For block-constant a, yes. `M = max(v current convolution)`. Then `S@v` ≤ M? S@v = c? Let's verify: S[t,k] = sum_{i in block k} a[t-i]; multiply by v_k (a_i) and sum gives Σ_i a_i a[t-i] = c_t. So exactly c (within float). Use M = max(c) + eps. Good.

If due to FFT error, S@v might be slightly larger than c. We can compute M = max(c) and also `M = max(M, np.max(S @ v) + 1e-12)`? Computing S@v costs m? Use dot. That ensures feasibility. But S@v should be c. Do:
```

```

Now, explicit 3-step baseline with n not divisible by m? We can make a general sequence:
```
def make_three_step(n):
    l1 = n//3
    l3 = n//3
    l2 = n - l1 - l3
    lengths = np.array([l1,l2,l3])
    v = np.array([3.0,2.0,2.0])
    total = v @ lengths
    v = v/total
    a = np.repeat(v, lengths)
    return a
```
This has high at left, low middle and right. Equivalent to [3,2,2] proportions. If n=3, q exact. For n=10240, good.
Maybe [2,2,3] same score due reversal? Convolution same if reversed, but [3,2,2] reversed is [2,2,3]. Since objective invariant under reversal, both same. Our block LP may choose orientation; if it flips, okay.

In `optimize_block_fixed`, we need rng for random fallbacks. Pass `rng`.
```
def optimize_block_fixed(a, lengths, deadline, rng, max_iters=200, print_tag=""):
    v = block_values_from_array(a, lengths)
    v = normalize_block_values(v, lengths)
    a = make_block_array(v, lengths)
    score = fast_score(a)
    best_a = a.copy(); best_score=score
    stuck=0
    alphas = [0.5,0.25,0.1,0.6,0.15,0.05,0.02]
    for it in range(max_iters):
       if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
       c = fast_convolve(a)
       Lc = c.size
       M = float(c.max()) if c.size else 0
       if M <=0: break
       S = build_S_for_blocks(a, lengths)
       # M robust
       conv_from_S = S @ v
       if conv_from_S.size:
          M2 = float(conv_from_S.max())
          if M2 > M: M = M2
       M = M * (1+1e-8) + 1e-300
       g = None
       if linprog is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 5:
           # time limit
           tl = min(20.0, max(2.0, (deadline - time.monotonic())/2.0))
           g = solve_block_lp(S, lengths, M, time_limit=tl)
       if g is None:
           # topK fallback
           K = max(1, min(Lc, Lc//200))
           if K >= Lc: idx = np.arange(Lc)
           else: idx = np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]
           w = np.zeros(Lc); w[idx]=1.0/K
           grad = 2.0 * (S.T @ w)
           # additive direction
           scale = np.max(np.abs(grad))
           if scale <=0: h = v
           else: h = v - (1.0/scale) * grad
           h = np.maximum(h,0)
           htot = h @ lengths
           if htot <=0: h = v
           else: dir_arr = make_block_array(h/htot, lengths)
       else:
           dir_arr = make_block_array(g, lengths)
           if dir_arr is None: dir_arr = a.copy()
       # line search
       improved = False
       best_local_score = score
       best_local_a = None
       for alpha in alphas:
           cand = (1-alpha)*a + alpha*dir_arr
           candn = normalize_nonneg(cand)
           if candn is None: continue
           sc = fast_score(candn)
           if sc < best_local_score - 1e-12:
               best_local_score = sc; best_local_a = candn
       if best_local_a is not None:
           v = block_values_from_array(best_local_a, lengths)
           v = normalize_block_values(v, lengths)
           a = make_block_array(v, lengths)
           score = best_local_score
           if score < best_score - 1e-10:
               best_score=score; best_a=a.copy()
           stuck=0
           if it % 10 ==0: print(...)
       else:
           stuck +=1
           # occasionally random perturbation
           if stuck == 10 or stuck % 20 == 0:
              pert = v * np.exp(rng.normal(0,0.1,len(v)))
              pert = np.maximum(pert, 0)
              ptot = pert @ lengths
              if ptot>0:
                  dau = make_block_array(pert/ptot, lengths)
                  sc = fast_score(dau)
                  if sc < score:
                      ...
           if stuck > 40: break
    return best_a, best_score
```

```

```
def resample_sequence(a, new_n):
    old_n=a.size
    x_old=np.linspace(0,1,old_n,endpoint=False?)
    x_new=np.linspace(0,1,new_n,endpoint=False)
    return np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
```

```
def polish_full(a, deadline, rng):
    a = normalize_nonneg(a)
    if a is None: return a
    best_a = a.copy(); best_score = fast_score(a)
    n = a.size
    Ks = [1,2,4,8,16,32,64]
    # filter K <= len(c)
    lr_scales = [0.005,0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1]
    eta_scales = [0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2]
    # step through Ks cyclically, with counts based on time
    K_cycle = [1,2,4,8,16,32,64,32,16,8]
    for it in range(10000):
       if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
       K = K_cycle[it % len(K_cycle)]
       if K * 10 > n: K = max(1,n//10)
       c = fast_convolve(a)
       if K >= c.size: idx=np.arange(c.size)
       else: idx=np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]
       w = np.zeros(c.size); w[idx]=1.0/K
       L = load_from_weights(a,w)
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(L)): continue
       # If L all zero, break
       improved=False
       trial_scores=[]
       # additive trials maybe 2
       for scale in [0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2]:
           lr = scale * n
           cand = a - 2.0*lr*L
           candn = normalize_nonneg(cand)
           if candn is None: continue
           sc = fast_score(candn)
           if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
               best_score=sc; best_a=candn.copy(); improved=True
       if not improved:
           for scale in [0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.5]:
               eta = scale * n
               cand = a / (1.0 + eta*L)
               candn = normalize_nonneg(cand)
               if candn is None: continue
               sc = fast_score(candn)
               if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
                   best_score=sc; best_a=candn.copy(); improved=True; break
       if improved:
           # print occasionally
           pass
       else:
           # after many, reduce K? just continue; if 200 no improve break
    return best_a
```

Need `load_from_weights` function:
```
def load_from_weights(a,w):
    n=a.size
    if n == 1:
       return np.array([w[0]*a[0]]) # c0=a0^2 derivative? L=a0? Actually L[i]=Σ_t w[t]a[t-i]; for n=1, a[0].
    Lc=2*n-1
    N=next_pow2(Lc + n -1) # 3n-2
    fa=np.fft.rfft(a,N)
    fw=np.fft.rfft(w[::-1],N)
    full=np.fft.irfft(fa*fw,N)
    return full[n-1:2*n-1][::-1]
```
Check orientation: earlier grad/2 = L_i = Σ_t w_t a[t-i]. full=conv(w_rev,a); L_i=full[2n-2-i]. Slice start=2n-2-(n-1)=n-1, stop=2n-1 exclusive? For i=n-1 index=2n-2-(n-1)=n-1. For i=0 index=2n-2. So full[n-1:2n-1] has indices n-1,...,2n-2; reversed gives i=0...n-1? full[n-1:2n-1][::-1] first element full[2n-2] (i=0), last full[n-1] (i=n-1). Good.
For n=2, full[1:3][::-1] indices2,1. Good.

Need maybe clip tiny negatives in L? L can have small negative due toFFT; but a,w nonnegative, convolution should nonnegative; clip `np.maximum(full,0)`.

`fast_convolve` orientation: We need c_t=Σ_i a_i a[t-i]. DFT product fx*fx gives linear convolution a*a (same). For real arrays, `irfft(fx*fx)` yields c. Good.

Detailed helper implementations:

```
def next_pow2(x):
    n=1
    while n < x:
        n <<=1
    return n
```

`normalize_nonneg`:
```
def normalize_nonneg(x, target=1.0):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    if x.size == 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
        return None
    x = np.where(x < 0.0, 0.0, x)
    s = float(x.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return None
    x = x * (target/s)
    # cap to 1000? internal not necessary; if target=1, max <=1. For raw maybe cap.
    if np.any(x > 1000.0): x = np.minimum(x,1000.0)
    return x
```
If target=1, cap no effect. If x has large due to raw, cap before normalize? Evaluator clips negatives and caps after; but we don't need raw. In `make_block_array`, v normalized.

`fast_convolve`:
```
def fast_convolve(x):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    n=x.size
    if n==0: return np.array([])
    L=2*n-1
    N=next_pow2(L)
    fx=np.fft.rfft(x, N)
    out=np.fft.irfft(fx*fx, N)[:L]
    out = np.where(out > 0.0, out, 0.0)  # small negative
    return out
```

`fast_score`:
```
def fast_score(x):
    x=np.asarray(x,float)
    if x.size==0: return np.inf
    s = x.sum()
    if s <= 1e-12: return np.inf
    y = x/s
    c = fast_convolve(y)
    if c.size==0: return np.inf
    m = float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(m): return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * y.size * m)
```
Since sum=1.

`make_lengths`:
```
def make_lengths(n, m):
    n=int(n); m=int(m)
    if m<=0: m=1
    if m>=n: m=n
    base = n // m
    rem = n % m
    lengths = np.full(m, base, dtype=np.int64)
    if rem>0:
        lengths[:rem] += 1
    return lengths
```

`block_values_from_array`:
```
def block_values_from_array(a, lengths):
    a=np.asarray(a,float); lengths=np.asarray(lengths,int64)
    m=lengths.size; v=np.zeros(m,float)
    start=0
    for k in range(m):
        end=start+int(lengths[k])
        if end>start:
            v[k]=float(np.mean(a[start:end]))
        start=end
    return v
```
If lengths sum != a.size, this may fail. Ensure called with matching lengths.

`normalize_block_values`:
```
def normalize_block_values(v, lengths):
    v=np.asarray(v,float).copy(); lengths=np.asarray(lengths,int64)
    v=np.where(v>0, v, 0)
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(v)): v=np.ones_like(v)
    total=float(v @ lengths)
    if total <= 1e-15:
       v = np.ones_like(v, float)
       total = float(v @ lengths)
    v = v / total
    return v
```

`make_block_array`:
```
def make_block_array(v, lengths):
    v = normalize_block_values(v, lengths)
    return np.repeat(v, lengths)
```

Initial functions:
```
def initial_v_three(m, lengths):
    t = (np.arange(m, dtype=np.float64)+0.5)/float(max(1,m))
    v = np.where(t < 1.0/3.0, 3.0, 2.0)
    return normalize_block_values(v, lengths)
```
For m=1, all 3 but normalized same.

`initial_v_linear`:
```
def initial_v_linear(m, lengths, slope=0.5, intercept=1.0):
    t = np.linspace(0,1,m) if m>1 else np.array([0.0])
    v = intercept + slope * t? For decreasing slope negative.
    v = 1.0 - 0.5*t
    v = np.maximum(v,0.05)
    return normalize_block_values(v,lengths)
```

`initial_v_random`:
```
def initial_v_random(m, lengths, rng):
    v = rng.random(m) + 0.1
    return normalize_block_values(v,lengths)
```

`resample_sequence_to_lengths(a, lengths)`: if sum lengths == a.size:
   v=block_values_from_array(a,lengths); return make_block_array(v,lengths)
Else use linear interpolation:
```
def resample_sequence_to_lengths(a, lengths):
    target_n=sum(lengths)
    a=np.asarray(a,float)
    if a.size == target_n:
       return make_block_array(block_values_from_array(a,lengths), lengths)
    if a.size == 0: return None
    x_old=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,a.size,endpoint=False)
    x_new=np.linspace(0.0,1.0,target_n,endpoint=False)
    new_full=np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
    return make_block_array(block_values_from_array(new_full,lengths), lengths)
```
This can resample across n.

`get_exact_score` as above.

`solve_block_lp` need import linprog global. Use bounds list. To avoid huge `bounds` allocation each time, okay.
```
def solve_block_lp(S, lengths, M, time_limit=10.0):
    if linprog is None: return None
    if S.size ==0 or M <=0: return None
    row_sum = S.sum(axis=1)
    rows = np.where(row_sum > 1e-300)[0]
    if rows.size ==0: return None
    S2 = S[rows,:] if rows.size != S.shape[0] else S
    b2 = np.full(S2.shape[0], M, dtype=np.float64)
    c = -np.asarray(lengths, dtype=np.float64)
    mvar = S2.shape[1]
    bounds = [(0.0, None)] * mvar
    # If many constraints, maybe random subset if time limit very short
    if S2.shape[0] > 12000 and time_limit < 5.0:
       rng = np.random.default_rng(0) # deterministic? But top-level no closure. Could use np.random.
       # But subset may miss constraints. Keep all.
    try:
       options = {}
       if time_limit > 0:
           options['time_limit'] = float(max(1.0, time_limit))
       options['presolve'] = True
       res = linprog(c, A_ub=S2, b_ub=b2, bounds=bounds, method='highs', options=options)
       if getattr(res, 'success', False):
          x = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
          if x.size == mvar and np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
             x = np.maximum(x, 0.0)
             if x.sum() > 1e-300:
                return x
    except Exception:
       pass
    return None
```
`options['presolve']` maybe not supported? In SciPy Highs, option is `presolve`? I think yes. To be safe, use `options={'time_limit': ...}` only; presolve default. If invalid option raises? SciPy may ignore? It might pass to HiGHS and error if unknown? I think 'presolve' valid. But to minimize risk, use only `time_limit`. However, 'time_limit' also valid? In scipy optimize._linprog_highs, options can include 'time_limit', 'presolve', etc. Should be fine. Could omit options entirely to avoid errors. But if an LP takes >100s, might eat time. Set `time_limit` likely safe. Let's use `options={'time_limit': float(max(1.0, time_limit))}` only. If time_limit=0, no options.

`build_S_for_blocks` as above. Need ensure `lengths` int64.

`optimize_block_fixed` needs access to `deadline`, `rng`, `max_iters`, `print_tag`. Let's write carefully.
```
def optimize_block_fixed(a, lengths, deadline, rng, max_iters=2000, print_tag=""):
    a0 = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    lengths = np.asarray(lengths, dtype=np.int64)
    # ensure block constant by extracting values
    v = block_values_from_array(a0, lengths)
    v = normalize_block_values(v, lengths)
    a = make_block_array(v, lengths)
    score = fast_score(a)
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = score
    alphas = (0.5,0.25,0.1,0.6,0.15,0.05,0.02)
    stuck = 0
    improvement_count = 0
    if print_tag:
       print(f"{print_tag} start score={score:.10f}", flush=True)
    for it in range(max_iters):
       if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
       c = fast_convolve(a)
       ncons = c.size
       if ncons == 0: break
       M = float(c.max())
       if M <= 1e-300: break
       S = build_S_for_blocks(a, lengths)
       # robust M
       Sv = S @ v
       Msv = float(np.max(Sv)) if Sv.size else M
       if Msv > M: M = Msv
       M *= (1.0 + 1e-8); M += 1e-300
       g = None
       if linprog is not None and (deadline - time.monotonic()) > 3.0:
           tl = min(15.0, max(1.5, (deadline - time.monotonic()) * 0.25))
           g = solve_block_lp(S, lengths, M, time_limit=tl)
       if g is not None and float(g.sum()) > 0:
           dir_a = make_block_array(g, lengths)
       else:
           K = max(1, min(ncons, max(1, ncons // 200)))
           if K >= ncons:
               idx = np.arange(ncons)
           else:
               idx = np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]
           w = np.zeros(ncons, dtype=np.float64)
           w[idx] = 1.0 / float(K)
           grad = 2.0 * (S.T @ w)
           scale = float(np.max(np.abs(grad))) if grad.size else 0.0
           if scale > 0:
               h = v - (1.0 / scale) * grad
           else:
               h = v
           dir_a = make_block_array(h, lengths)
       if dir_a is None:
           dir_a = a
       # line search
       local_best_score = score
       local_best_a = None
       for alpha in alphas:
           cand = (1.0 - alpha) * a + alpha * dir_a
           cand = normalize_nonneg(cand)
           if cand is None: continue
           sc = fast_score(cand)
           if sc < local_best_score - 1e-12:
               local_best_score = sc
               local_best_a = cand
       if local_best_a is not None:
           v = block_values_from_array(local_best_a, lengths)
           v = normalize_block_values(v, lengths)
           a = make_block_array(v, lengths)
           score = local_best_score
           if score < best_score - 1e-10:
               best_score = score
               best_a = a.copy()
           improvement_count += 1
           stuck = 0
           if print_tag and (improvement_count % 10 == 0):
               print(f"{print_tag} iter={it} improved={improvement_count} score={best_score:.10f} time_left={max(0,deadline-time.monotonic()):.1f}", flush=True)
       else:
           stuck += 1
           if stuck in (10, 30):
               # random perturbation
               for sigma in (0.1,0.03):
                   pert = v * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, v.size))
                   pert = np.maximum(pert, 0.0)
                   ptot = float(pert @ lengths)
                   if ptot > 0:
                       ra = make_block_array(pert / ptot, lengths)
                       sc = fast_score(ra)
                       if sc < score - 1e-12:
                           v = block_values_from_array(ra, lengths); v=normalize_block_values(v,lengths); a=make_block_array(v,lengths); score=sc
                           break
           if stuck > 50:
               break
    if print_tag:
       print(f"{print_tag} done score={best_score:.10f}", flush=True)
    return best_a, best_score
```
Potential: `g` per-position values from LP can have very different scale; `make_block_array` normalizes. Good.
`dir_a` from fallback `h` per-position; normalize. Good.
Line search: If `local_best_a` is from a small alpha, we enforce block constant via v means. Good.
If alphas list includes 0.6 then 0.1 etc order; we pick any best. Could add 0.8. Fine.

Time: Each iteration builds S and maybe LP. If LP fails often due to time, fallback uses no LP but S.T@w. Good.

`stage_optimize`:
```
def stage_optimize(a_current, n, m, lengths, stage_deadline, rng, tag=""):
    best_a = None; best_sc = np.inf
    starts = []
    if a_current is not None and a_current.size == n:
        starts.append(resample_sequence_to_lengths(a_current, lengths))
    starts.append(make_block_array(initial_v_three(m, lengths), lengths))
    starts.append(make_block_array(initial_v_linear(m, lengths), lengths))
    # maybe random if time
    for idx, start in enumerate(starts):
        if time.monotonic() >= stage_deadline: break
        if start is None: continue
        sub_dead = stage_deadline
        # if multiple starts, we might want to give each part of remaining.
        # Simple: run start until stage_deadline if first; for subsequent only if time_left > 20.
        # But if first runs to stage_deadline, no time for others.
        a, sc = optimize_block_fixed(start, lengths, sub_dead, rng, print_tag=f"{tag}s{idx}")
        if sc < best_sc:
            best_sc=sc; best_a=a.copy()
        if time.monotonic() >= stage_deadline - 5: break
    return best_a, best_sc
```

Maybe we should allocate sub_dead per start proportional. For first current use 70% of stage if any, second 100%? Simpler:
- If current provided: run it until stage_deadline * 0.6 from stage_start; then run three/linear with remaining.

       update best

`pipeline`:
```
def run_pipeline(n, m_list, stage_deadline, rng, current_a=None):
    start_time = time.monotonic()
    total = max(0, stage_deadline - start_time)
    best_a = current_a
    for idx,m in enumerate(m_list):
        lengths = make_lengths(n,m)
        if current_a is not None and current_a.size == n:
            start_a = resample_sequence_to_lengths(current_a, lengths)
        else:
            start_a = make_block_array(initial_v_three(m,lengths), lengths)
        # stage deadline: fractions cumulative, but ensure monotonic
        frac = (idx+1)/len(m_list)
        stage_dead = start_time + total * min(1.0, frac)
        if idx == len(m_list)-1:
            stage_dead = stage_deadline
        # If stage_dead < now+10, use stage_deadline? For last.
        if stage_dead < time.monotonic() + 10:
            stage_dead = stage_deadline
        a, sc = optimize_block_fixed(start_a, lengths, stage_dead, rng, print_tag=f"pipe n={n} m={m}")
        if a is not None:
            current_a = a.copy()
            best_a = a.copy()
        if time.monotonic() >= stage_deadline: break
    return best_a
```
This gives equal fractions cumulative. If n=10368,m_list [3,6,12,24,48], stage_dead after first = start+0.2total. If m=3 finishes early, time wasted until second stage. def run_pipeline(n, m_list, deadline, rng, current_a=None):
    start_time = time.monotonic()
    total = max(0.0, deadline - start_time)
    best_a = current_a.copy() if current_a is not None else None
    best_sc = fast_score(best_a) if best_a is not None else np.inf
    start_time = time.monotonic()

        lengths=make_lengths(n,m)

        else:
            start_a = make_block_array(initial_v_three(m, lengths), lengths)
        if idx == len(m_list)-1:
            stage_dead = deadline
        else:
            stage_dead = start_time + total * ((idx+1)/float(len(m_list)))
        if stage_dead < time.monotonic() + 15:
            stage_dead = deadline
        a, sc = optimize_block_fixed(start_a, lengths, stage_dead, rng, print_tag=f"pipe n={n} m={m}")

            best_a = a.copy(); best_sc = sc
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
        # If finished stage early and there is significant time before next stage deadline, maybe random restart same m

        if time.monotonic() < stage_dead - 30: # actually stage_dead is current stage end, if optimize returned early
            # try 3-step start for same m using remaining until next stage or stage_dead?
            remaining_dead = min(stage_dead, next_base)???
```

Now, full polish function. We need make efficient. Let's implement a simpler polish with fewer trials to avoid too many FFTs.
```
def polish_full(a, deadline, rng):
    a = normalize_nonneg(a)
    if a is None: return None
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = fast_score(a)
    n = a.size
    Kschedule = [1,2,4,8,16,32,64,128]
    scale_add = (0.01,0.03,0.08,0.15)
    scale_mult = (0.03,0.08,0.2,0.4)
    stuck=0
    it=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline and stuck < 300:
       it +=1
       K = Kschedule[it % len(Kschedule)]
       c = fast_convolve(a)
       if c.size ==0: break
       if K >= c.size: idx = np.arange(c.size)
       else: idx = np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]
       w = np.zeros(c.size); w[idx]=1.0/K
       L = load_from_weights(a,w)
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(L)): break
       # If L max zero, break
       improved=False
       # Trials: maybe only if time_left high do 4, else 2
       time_left = deadline - time.monotonic()
       if time_left > 200:
          add_scales = scale_add; mult_scales=scale_mult
       else:
          add_scales = scale_add[:2]; mult_scales=mult_scales[:2]
       for s in add_scales:
           cand = a - 2.0 * s * n * L
           cand = normalize_nonneg(cand)
           if cand is None: continue
           sc = fast_score(cand)
           if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
               best_score=sc; best_a=cand.copy(); improved=True
       if not improved:
           for s in mult_scales:
               cand = a / (1.0 + s * n * L)
               cand = normalize_nonneg(cand)
               if cand is None: continue
               sc = fast_score(cand)
               if sc < best_score - 1e-12:
                   best_score=sc; best_a=cand.copy(); improved=True; break
       if improved:
           stuck=0
           if it % 20 ==0: print...
       else:
           stuck +=1
           if stuck == 50:
               # random perturbation
               cand = a * (1.0 + 0.02*rng.normal(size=n))
               cand = normalize_nonneg(cand)
               if cand is not None:
                   sc=fast_score(cand)
                   if sc < best_score:
                      best_score=sc; best_a=cand.copy(); improved=True
    return best_a
```
Potential issue: `a - 2*s*n*L` if s=0.15,n=10000,L~1e-4 => step ~0.3, may make many negative; but normalize clips; maybe too aggressive. We can reduce scales: [0.002,0.005,0.01,0.02] since s*n gives step fraction. Let's recalc: Want step ~ fraction f of a. L~a~1/n. 2*s*n*L ~2s. If s=0.01 => step 0.02 (2%); s=0.1 => step0.2. So scales [0.002,0.005,0.01,0.02] are safe. Multiplicative eta = s*n*L ~s; s [0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2]. Good.
Use additive scales (0.002,0.005,0.01,0.02,0.05) and mult (0.02,0.05,0.1,0.2,0.4).
Step `a - 2.0 * add_scale * n * L`. Good.

`load_from_weights` clip negatives? Return `np.maximum(full[slice],0)`.

Potential issue: The LP constraints `S @ g <= M` are linear, but because g per-position values and lengths, if some block length large, objective weights it, constraints coefficients independent of length? S coefficients sum a over other indices; variable g_k multiplied by length in objective but not in constraints. In full sequence variables, if we restrict all positions in block to same value g_k, constraint coefficient for that value is S_tk (sum over other indices for each variable position in block? Wait if all positions i in block k have value g_k, contribution to (a*g)_t from these positions is g_k * Σ_{i in block k} a_{t-i} = S_tk * g_k. So constraint coefficient is S_tk, independent of length (S already sums over positions in block). Objective sum over positions is Σ_i g_i = length_k * g_k. So objective c=-lengths. Correct.

Now, the full LP's feasible set includes g=a? If a block values v_k, constraints S@v = c ≤ M; objective lengths@v =1. Good.

Let's write `run_pipeline` with m_list and stage fractions. It needs `current_a` maybe None. It should not call exact.

```
def run_pipeline(n, m_list, deadline, rng, current_a=None):
    start_time = time.monotonic()
    total = max(0.0, deadline - start_time)
    if total < 10: return current_a
    best_a = current_a.copy() if current_a is not None else None
    for idx, m in enumerate(m_list):
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now >= deadline: break
        lengths = make_lengths(n, m)
        if best_a is not None and best_a.size == n:
            start_a = resample_sequence_to_lengths(best_a, lengths)
        else:
            start_a = make_block_array(initial_v_three(m, lengths), lengths)
        if idx == len(m_list)-1:
            stage_dead = deadline
        else:
            stage_dead = start_time + total * ((idx + 1) / float(len(m_list)))
        if stage_dead < now + 15:
            stage_dead = deadline
        a, sc = optimize_block_fixed(start_a, lengths, stage_dead, rng, print_tag=f"pipe n={n} m={m}")
        if a is not None:
            best_a = a.copy()
        # If early, optional alt start
        if a is not None and time.monotonic() + 45 < stage_dead:
            # try linear init for same m
            alt_a = make_block_array(initial_v_linear(m, lengths), lengths)
            if alt_a.size == n:
                a2, sc2 = optimize_block_fixed(alt_a, lengths, stage_dead, rng, print_tag=f"pipe n={n} m={m} alt")
                if a2 is not None:
                    # Need decide if sc2 < sc? optimize returns sc. Compare fast_score?
                    if fast_score(a2) < fast_score(a):
                        a = a2
            if a is not None:
                best_a = a.copy()
        if time.monotonic() >= deadline: break
    return best_a
```
But if first `optimize` returns early because stuck, `a` score maybe. Alt may improve. If first used most time, no alt.

`make_lengths` if m>=n: m=n, base=1, rem=0. If n=0? not.

`resample_sequence_to_lengths`:
```
def resample_sequence_to_lengths(a, lengths):
    lengths = np.asarray(lengths, dtype=np.int64)
    target_n = int(lengths.sum())
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if target_n <= 0 or a.size == 0:
        return None
    if a.size == target_n:
        v = block_values_from_array(a, lengths)
        return make_block_array(v, lengths)
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, a.size, endpoint=False)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, target_n, endpoint=False)
    new_full = np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
    v = block_values_from_array(new_full, lengths)
    return make_block_array(v, lengths)
```
If a contains negatives? Interp negative; make_block normalize clips.

`initial_v_three` for m large: t centers. If m=1, t<1/3 true, v=3. Good.

`initial_v_linear`:
```
def initial_v_linear(m, lengths, stretch=0.5):
    if m == 1: v=np.ones(1)
    else: t=np.linspace(0,1,m); v=1.0 - stretch*t
    v=np.maximum(v,0.05)
    return normalize_block_values(v,lengths)
```

Now, final code for `polish_full`:
```
def polish_full(a, deadline, rng):
    a = normalize_nonneg(a)
    if a is None: return None
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = fast_score(a)
    n = a.size
    Kschedule = (1,2,4,8,16,32,64,128)
    mult_scales = (0.01, 0.03, 0.1, 0.3)
    add_scales = (1e-6, 1e-5, 3e-5, 1e-4)
    stuck=0; it=0
    while time.monotonic() < deadline and stuck < 400:
       it += 1
       K = Kschedule[(it-1) % len(Kschedule)]
       c = fast_convolve(best_a)
       csize = c.size
       if csize == 0: break
       if K >= csize: idx=np.arange(csize)
       else: idx=np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]
       w=np.zeros(csize)
       topv = c[idx]
       sump = float(topv.sum())
       if sump <=0: break
       w[idx] = topv / sump
       L = load_from_weights(best_a, w)
       if not np.all(np.isfinite(L)): break
       L = np.maximum(L,0)
       lmax = float(L.max()) if L.size else 0
       if lmax <= 0: break
       improved=False
       time_left = deadline - time.monotonic()
       # mult trials
       if time_left > 100:
           ms = mult_scales
       else:
           ms = mult_scales[:2]
       for sc in ms:
           cand = best_a / (1.0 + sc * float(n) * L)
           cand = normalize_nonneg(cand)
           if cand is None: continue
           s = fast_score(cand)
           if s < best_score - 1e-12:
               best_score=s; best_a=cand.copy(); improved=True; break
       if not improved:
           if time_left > 100:
               as = add_scales
           else:
               as = add_scales[:2]
           for sc in as:
               cand = best_a - 2.0 * sc * float(n) * L
               cand = normalize_nonneg(cand)
               if cand is None: continue
               s = fast_score(cand)
               if s < best_score - 1e-12:
                   best_score=s; best_a=cand.copy(); improved=True; break
       if improved:
           stuck=0
           if it % 25 == 0: print(...)
       else:
           stuck += 1
           if stuck == 100:
               # random small multiplicative perturbation
               cand = best_a * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.02, best_a.size))
               cand = normalize_nonneg(cand)
               if cand is not None:
                   s = fast_score(cand)
                   if s < best_score:
                       best_score=s; best_a=cand.copy(); improved=True
    return best_a
```
This may break after 400 no improve. Good.

def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    start = time.monotonic()
    try:
        budget = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget = 1800.0
    if budget <= 5: budget = 5.0
    deadline = start + max(1.0, budget - 12.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)
    candidates=[]
    best_internal_a = None
    best_internal = np.inf

    # parent optional
    try:
        p = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
        if p.ndim ==1 and p.size>0 and np.all(np.isfinite(p)):
            pn = normalize_nonneg(p)
            if pn is not None:
                candidates.append(pn)
                if fast_score(pn) < best_internal:
                    best_internal = fast_score(pn); best_internal_a = pn.copy()
    except Exception:
        pass

    # baseline n values
    baseline_ns = (10368, 6962)
    for n in baseline_ns:
       lengths=make_lengths(n,3)
       v=initial_v_three(3,lengths)
       a=make_block_array(v,lengths)
       if a is None: continue
       sc=fast_score(a)
       if sc < best_internal:
           best_internal=sc; best_internal_a=a.copy()
       if len(candidates) < 6: candidates.append(a.copy())
       # print
```

def run_single_block_task(current_a, n, m, deadline, rng, tag=""):
    lengths = make_lengths(n,m)
    if current_a is not None and current_a.size == n:
        start_a = resample_sequence_to_lengths(current_a, lengths)
    else:
        start_a = make_block_array(initial_v_three(m, lengths), lengths)
    if start_a is None or time.monotonic() >= deadline:
        return None
    a, sc = optimize_block_fixed(start_a, lengths, deadline, rng, print_tag=tag)
    if a is not None and time.monotonic() + 40 < deadline:
        alt_v = initial_v_linear(m, lengths)
        alt_a = make_block_array(alt_v, lengths)
        a2, sc2 = optimize_block_fixed(alt_a, lengths, deadline, rng, print_tag=tag + " alt")
        if a2 is not None:
            s1 = fast_score(a); s2=fast_score(a2)
            if s2 < s1:
                a = a2
    return a
```

```python
"""
Searches nonnegative step-function sequences for low value of
2*n*max(convolve(a,a))/(sum(a)**2).

Strategy:
- Keep working in the simplex sum(a)=1, so the score is 2*n*max(a*a).
- Build strong piecewise-constant candidates (step functions).
- Optimize piecewise-constant block values with an LP-based direction
  inspired by the previous state of the art:
      maximize sum(g) subject to a*g <= max(a*a), g >= 0
  then blend a with normalized g and keep exact-improving candidates.
- Refine with top-constraint subgradient / multiplicative updates on the
  full sequence.
"""

import numpy as np
import time

try:
    from scipy.optimize import linprog
except Exception:
    linprog = None


def next_pow2(x):
    n = 1
    x = int(max(1, x))
    while n < x:
        n <<= 1
    return n


def normalize_nonneg(x, target=1.0):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    if x.size == 0 or not np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
        return None
    x = np.where(x < 0.0, 0.0, x)
    s = float(x.sum())
    if s <= 1e-15:
        return None
    x = x * (float(target) / s)
    if np.any(x > 1000.0):
        x = np.minimum(x, 1000.0)
    return x


def fast_convolve(x):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    n = x.size
    if n == 0:
        return np.array([], dtype=np.float64)
    L = 2 * n - 1
    N = next_pow2(L)
    fx = np.fft.rfft(x, N)
    out = np.fft.irfft(fx * fx, N)[:L]
    out = np.where(out > 0.0, out, 0.0)
    return out


def fast_score(x):
    x = np.asarray(x, dtype=np.float64)
    if x.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    s = float(x.sum())
    if s <= 1e-12:
        return np.inf
    y = x / s
    c = fast_convolve(y)
    if c.size == 0:
        return np.inf
    m = float(c.max())
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return np.inf
    return float(2.0 * float(y.size) * m)


def make_lengths(n, m):
    n = int(max(1, n))
    m = int(max(1, m))
    if m > n:
        m = n
    base = n // m
    rem = n % m
    lengths = np.full(m, base, dtype=np.int64)
    if rem:
        lengths[:rem] += 1
    return lengths


def block_values_from_array(a, lengths):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    lengths = np.asarray(lengths, dtype=np.int64)
    m = lengths.size
    v = np.zeros(m, dtype=np.float64)
    start = 0
    for k in range(m):
        end = start + int(lengths[k])
        if end > start:
            v[k] = float(np.mean(a[start:end]))
        start = end
    return v


def normalize_block_values(v, lengths):
    v = np.asarray(v, dtype=np.float64).copy()
    lengths = np.asarray(lengths, dtype=np.int64)
    v = np.where(np.isfinite(v) & (v > 0.0), v, 0.0)
    if not np.any(v > 0.0):
        v = np.ones(v.size, dtype=np.float64)
    total = float(v @ lengths)
    if total <= 1e-15:
        v = np.ones(v.size, dtype=np.float64)
        total = float(v @ lengths)
    return v / total


def make_block_array(v, lengths):
    v = normalize_block_values(v, lengths)
    return np.repeat(v, lengths)


def resample_sequence_to_lengths(a, lengths):
    lengths = np.asarray(lengths, dtype=np.int64)
    target_n = int(lengths.sum())
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    if target_n <= 0 or a.size == 0:
        return None
    if a.size == target_n:
        v = block_values_from_array(a, lengths)
        return make_block_array(v, lengths)
    x_old = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, a.size, endpoint=False)
    x_new = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, target_n, endpoint=False)
    new_full = np.interp(x_new, x_old, a)
    v = block_values_from_array(new_full, lengths)
    return make_block_array(v, lengths)


def initial_v_three(m, lengths):
    m = int(max(1, m))
    t = (np.arange(m, dtype=np.float64) + 0.5) / float(m)
    v = np.where(t < (1.0 / 3.0), 3.0, 2.0)
    return normalize_block_values(v, lengths)


def initial_v_linear(m, lengths):
    m = int(max(1, m))
    if m == 1:
        v = np.ones(1, dtype=np.float64)
    else:
        t = np.linspace(0.0, 1.0, m, dtype=np.float64)
        v = 1.0 - 0.5 * t
    v = np.maximum(v, 0.05)
    return normalize_block_values(v, lengths)


def build_S_for_blocks(a, lengths):
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    lengths = np.asarray(lengths, dtype=np.int64)
    n = a.size
    m = lengths.size
    L = 2 * n - 1
    t = np.arange(L, dtype=np.int64)

    prefix = np.empty(n + 1, dtype=np.float64)
    prefix[0] = 0.0
    np.cumsum(a, out=prefix[1:])

    S = np.empty((L, m), dtype=np.float64)
    low = np.empty(L, dtype=np.int64)
    high = np.empty(L, dtype=np.int64)
    vals = np.empty(L, dtype=np.float64)

    start = 0
    for k in range(m):
        end = start + int(lengths[k])
        np.maximum(0, t - (end - 1), out=low)
        np.minimum(n - 1, t - start, out=high)
        valid = high >= low
        vals.fill(0.0)
        if np.any(valid):
            vals[valid] = prefix[high[valid] + 1] - prefix[low[valid]]
        S[:, k] = vals
        start = end
    return S


def solve_block_lp(S, lengths, M, time_limit=10.0):
    if linprog is None:
        return None
    S = np.asarray(S, dtype=np.float64)
    if S.size == 0 or M <= 0.0 or not np.isfinite(M):
        return None

    row_sum = S.sum(axis=1)
    rows = np.where(row_sum > 1e-300)[0]
    if rows.size == 0:
        return None

    if rows.size != S.shape[0]:
        S2 = S[rows, :]
    else:
        S2 = S
    b2 = np.full(S2.shape[0], M, dtype=np.float64)
    c = -np.asarray(lengths, dtype=np.float64)
    mv = S2.shape[1]
    if mv <= 0:
        return None
    bounds = [(0.0, None)] * mv

    try:
        opts = {"time_limit": float(max(1.0, time_limit))}
        res = linprog(
            c,
            A_ub=S2,
            b_ub=b2,
            bounds=bounds,
            method="highs",
            options=opts,
        )
        if getattr(res, "success", False):
            x = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
            if x.size == mv and np.all(np.isfinite(x)):
                x = np.maximum(x, 0.0)
                if x.sum() > 1e-300:
                    return x
    except Exception:
        pass
    return None


def load_from_weights(a, w):
    """Returns L[i] = sum_t w[t] a[t-i], i.e. half the top-weight gradient."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)
    w = np.asarray(w, dtype=np.float64)
    n = a.size
    Lc = 2 * n - 1
    if n == 1:
        return np.array([w[0] * a[0]], dtype=np.float64)
    N = next_pow2(Lc + n - 1)
    fa = np.fft.rfft(a, N)
    fw = np.fft.rfft(w[::-1], N)
    full = np.fft.irfft(fa * fw, N)
    out = full[n - 1:2 * n - 1][::-1]
    return np.maximum(out, 0.0)


def get_exact_score(a):
    try:
        vals = [float(x) for x in np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)]
        val = float(evaluate_sequence(vals))
        if np.isfinite(val):
            return val
    except Exception:
        pass
    return np.inf


def optimize_block_fixed(a, lengths, deadline, rng, max_iters=20000, print_tag=""):
    lengths = np.asarray(lengths, dtype=np.int64)
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64)

    v = block_values_from_array(a, lengths)
    v = normalize_block_values(v, lengths)
    a = make_block_array(v, lengths)

    score = fast_score(a)
    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = score

    alphas = (0.5, 0.25, 0.1, 0.6, 0.15, 0.05, 0.02)
    stuck = 0
    improvements = 0
    lp_fail_streak = 0

    if print_tag:
        print(f"{print_tag}: start score={score:.10f}", flush=True)

    for it in range(max_iters):
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now >= deadline:
            break

        c = fast_convolve(a)
        ncons = c.size
        if ncons == 0:
            break
        M = float(c.max())
        if M <= 1e-300:
            break

        S = build_S_for_blocks(a, lengths)

        Sv = S @ v
        M2 = float(np.max(Sv)) if Sv.size else M
        if M2 > M:
            M = M2
        M = M * (1.0 + 1e-8) + 1e-300

        g = None
        if (linprog is not None and lp_fail_streak < 6 and (deadline - now) > 3.0):
            tl = min(15.0, max(1.5, (deadline - now) * 0.25))
            g = solve_block_lp(S, lengths, M, time_limit=tl)
            if g is None:
                lp_fail_streak += 1
            else:
                lp_fail_streak = 0

        if g is not None and float(g.sum()) > 0.0:
            dir_a = make_block_array(g, lengths)
        else:
            K = max(1, min(ncons, max(1, ncons // 200)))
            if K >= ncons:
                idx = np.arange(ncons)
            else:
                idx = np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]
            w = np.zeros(ncons, dtype=np.float64)
            top = c[idx]
            s = float(top.sum())
            if s <= 0.0:
                w[idx] = 1.0 / float(K)
            else:
                w[idx] = top / s

            loads = S.T @ w
            lmax = float(np.max(loads)) if loads.size else 0.0
            if lmax > 0.0:
                h = v / (1.0 + 10.0 * (loads / lmax))
            else:
                h = v
            dir_a = make_block_array(h, lengths)

        if dir_a is None:
            dir_a = a

        local_best_score = score
        local_best_a = None

        for alpha in alphas:
            cand = (1.0 - alpha) * a + alpha * dir_a
            cand = normalize_nonneg(cand)
            if cand is None:
                continue
            sc = fast_score(cand)
            if sc < local_best_score - 1e-12:
                local_best_score = sc
                local_best_a = cand

        if local_best_a is not None:
            v = block_values_from_array(local_best_a, lengths)
            v = normalize_block_values(v, lengths)
            a = make_block_array(v, lengths)
            score = local_best_score
            if score < best_score - 1e-10:
                best_score = score
                best_a = a.copy()
            improvements += 1
            stuck = 0
            if print_tag and (improvements % 10 == 0):
                print(
                    f"{print_tag}: iter={it} improvements={improvements} "
                    f"score={best_score:.10f} time_left={deadline-now:.1f}",
                    flush=True,
                )
        else:
            stuck += 1
            if stuck in (10, 30):
                sigma = 0.10 if stuck == 10 else 0.03
                pert = v * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, sigma, v.size))
                pert = np.maximum(pert, 0.0)
                ptot = float(pert @ lengths)
                if ptot > 0.0:
                    ra = make_block_array(pert / ptot, lengths)
                    sc = fast_score(ra)
                    if sc < score - 1e-12:
                        v = block_values_from_array(ra, lengths)
                        v = normalize_block_values(v, lengths)
                        a = make_block_array(v, lengths)
                        score = sc
            if stuck > 50:
                break

    if print_tag:
        print(f"{print_tag}: done score={best_score:.10f}", flush=True)
    return best_a, best_score


def run_pipeline(n, m_list, deadline, rng, current_a=None):
    start_time = time.monotonic()
    total = max(0.0, deadline - start_time)
    if total < 20.0:
        return current_a

    best_a = current_a.copy() if current_a is not None else None
    best_sc = fast_score(best_a) if best_a is not None else np.inf

    for idx, m in enumerate(m_list):
        now = time.monotonic()
        if now >= deadline:
            break

        lengths = make_lengths(n, m)
        if best_a is not None and best_a.size == n:
            start_a = resample_sequence_to_lengths(best_a, lengths)
        else:
            start_a = make_block_array(initial_v_three(m, lengths), lengths)
        if start_a is None:
            continue

        if idx == len(m_list) - 1:
            stage_dead = deadline
        else:
            stage_dead = start_time + total * ((idx + 1) / float(len(m_list)))
        if stage_dead < time.monotonic() + 15.0:
            stage_dead = deadline

        a, sc = optimize_block_fixed(
            start_a, lengths, stage_dead, rng,
            print_tag=f"pipe n={n} m={m}"
        )
        if a is not None:
            if sc < best_sc - 1e-12:
                best_sc = sc
                best_a = a.copy()
            else:
                best_a = a.copy()

        if a is not None and time.monotonic() + 45.0 < stage_dead:
            alt_a = make_block_array(initial_v_linear(m, lengths), lengths)
            if alt_a is not None and alt_a.size == n:
                a2, sc2 = optimize_block_fixed(
                    alt_a, lengths, stage_dead, rng,
                    print_tag=f"pipe n={n} m={m} alt"
                )
                if a2 is not None:
                    if sc2 < best_sc - 1e-12:
                        best_sc = sc2
                        best_a = a2.copy()
                    else:
                        s1 = fast_score(a)
                        s2 = fast_score(a2)
                        if s2 < s1:
                            best_a = a2.copy()
                            best_sc = s2

        if time.monotonic() >= deadline:
            break

    return best_a


def run_single_block_task(current_a, n, m, deadline, rng, tag=""):
    lengths = make_lengths(n, m)
    if current_a is not None and current_a.size == n:
        start_a = resample_sequence_to_lengths(current_a, lengths)
    else:
        start_a = make_block_array(initial_v_three(m, lengths), lengths)
    if start_a is None or time.monotonic() >= deadline:
        return None

    a, sc = optimize_block_fixed(start_a, lengths, deadline, rng, print_tag=tag)

    if a is not None and time.monotonic() + 40.0 < deadline:
        alt_a = make_block_array(initial_v_linear(m, lengths), lengths)
        if alt_a is not None and alt_a.size == n:
            a2, sc2 = optimize_block_fixed(
                alt_a, lengths, deadline, rng,
                print_tag=tag + " alt"
            )
            if a2 is not None:
                if sc2 < sc - 1e-12 or fast_score(a2) < fast_score(a):
                    a = a2
    return a


def polish_full(a, deadline, rng):
    a = normalize_nonneg(a)
    if a is None:
        return None

    best_a = a.copy()
    best_score = fast_score(a)
    n = a.size

    Kschedule = (1, 2, 4, 8, 16, 32, 64, 128)
    mult_scales = (0.01, 0.03, 0.10, 0.30)
    add_scales = (1e-6, 1e-5, 3e-5, 1e-4)

    stuck = 0
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline and stuck < 400:
        it += 1
        K = Kschedule[(it - 1) % len(Kschedule)]
        c = fast_convolve(best_a)
        csize = c.size
        if csize == 0:
            break

        if K >= csize:
            idx = np.arange(csize)
        else:
            idx = np.argpartition(c, -K)[-K:]

        w = np.zeros(csize, dtype=np.float64)
        top = c[idx]
        s = float(top.sum())
        if s <= 0.0:
            break
        w[idx] = top / s

        L = load_from_weights(best_a, w)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(L)):
            break
        L = np.maximum(L, 0.0)
        lmax = float(L.max()) if L.size else 0.0
        if lmax <= 0.0:
            break

        improved = False
        time_left = deadline - time.monotonic()

        if time_left > 120.0:
            ms = mult_scales
        else:
            ms = mult_scales[:2]

        for sc in ms:
            cand = best_a / (1.0 + sc * float(n) * L)
            cand = normalize_nonneg(cand)
            if cand is None:
                continue
            vals = fast_score(cand)
            if vals < best_score - 1e-12:
                best_score = vals
                best_a = cand.copy()
                improved = True
                break

        if not improved:
            if time_left > 120.0:
                ascales = add_scales
            else:
                ascales = add_scales[:2]
            for sc in ascales:
                cand = best_a - 2.0 * sc * float(n) * L
                cand = normalize_nonneg(cand)
                if cand is None:
                    continue
                vals = fast_score(cand)
                if vals < best_score - 1e-12:
                    best_score = vals
                    best_a = cand.copy()
                    improved = True
                    break

        if improved:
            stuck = 0
            if it % 25 == 0:
                print(
                    f"polish: it={it} score={best_score:.10f} "
                    f"time_left={deadline-time.monotonic():.1f}",
                    flush=True,
                )
        else:
            stuck += 1
            if stuck == 100:
                cand = best_a * np.exp(rng.normal(0.0, 0.02, best_a.size))
                cand = normalize_nonneg(cand)
                if cand is not None:
                    s = fast_score(cand)
                    if s < best_score - 1e-12:
                        best_score = s
                        best_a = cand.copy()
                        improved = True

    return best_a


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    start = time.monotonic()
    try:
        budget = float(budget_s)
    except Exception:
        budget = 1800.0
    if budget < 10.0:
        budget = 10.0
    deadline = start + max(1.0, budget - 12.0)
    rng = np.random.default_rng(seed)

    best_internal_a = None
    best_internal_score = np.inf
    candidates = []

    def consider(a):
        nonlocal best_internal_a, best_internal_score
        if a is None:
            return
        a = normalize_nonneg(a)
        if a is None:
            return
        s = fast_score(a)
        if np.isfinite(s) and s < best_internal_score - 1e-12:
            best_internal_score = s
            best_internal_a = a.copy()
            if len(candidates) < 10:
                candidates.append(a.copy())

    # Optional: consider parent if available, but do not rely on it.
    try:
        parent = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64)
        if parent.ndim == 1 and 0 < parent.size <= 50000 and np.all(np.isfinite(parent)):
            consider(parent)
    except Exception:
        pass

    # Strong explicit 3-step baselines: [3, 2, 2] proportions.
    for n0 in (10368, 6962):
        lengths = make_lengths(n0, 3)
        a0 = make_block_array(initial_v_three(3, lengths), lengths)
        consider(a0)

    init_budget = max(0.0, deadline - start)
    if init_budget < 60.0:
        if best_internal_a is None:
            best_internal_a = make_block_array(initial_v_three(3, make_lengths(10368, 3)), make_lengths(10368, 3))
        final_a = normalize_nonneg(best_internal_a)
        if final_a is None:
            final_a = np.ones(256, dtype=np.float64)
        return [float(x) for x in final_a]

    primary_deadline = start + 0.55 * init_budget
    m64_deadline = start + 0.70 * init_budget
    n2_deadline = start + 0.85 * init_budget
    m96_deadline = start + 0.92 * init_budget
    polish_deadline = deadline - 5.0

    n1 = 10368
    n2 = 6962

    if time.monotonic() < primary_deadline - 20.0:
        current = best_internal_a if (best_internal_a is not None and best_internal_a.size == n1) else None
        primary_a = run_pipeline(n1, [12, 24, 48], primary_deadline, rng, current_a=current)
        if primary_a is not None:
            consider(primary_a)
        else:
            primary_a = best_internal_a if best_internal_a is not None and best_internal_a.size == n1 else None
    else:
        primary_a = best_internal_a if (best_internal_a is not None and best_internal_a.size == n1) else None

    if time.monotonic() < m64_deadline - 30.0:
        a64 = run_single_block_task(primary_a, n1, 64, m64_deadline, rng, tag="n10368 m64")
        consider(a64)
        if a64 is not None:
            s64 = fast_score(a64)
            s1 = fast_score(primary_a) if primary_a is not None else np.inf
            if s64 < s1:
                primary_a = a64.copy()

    if time.monotonic() < n2_deadline - 30.0:
        a59 = run_single_block_task(primary_a, n2, 59, n2_deadline, rng, tag="n6962 m59")
        consider(a59)

    if time.monotonic() < m96_deadline - 30.0:
        a96 = run_single_block_task(primary_a, n1, 96, m96_deadline, rng, tag="n10368 m96")
        consider(a96)
        if a96 is not None:
            s96 = fast_score(a96)
            s1 = fast_score(primary_a) if primary_a is not None else np.inf
            if s96 < s1:
                primary_a = a96.copy()

    if best_internal_a is not None and time.monotonic() < polish_deadline - 20.0:
        pol = polish_full(best_internal_a, polish_deadline, rng)
        consider(pol)

    # Final authoritative check if there is comfortably enough time.
    final_a = best_internal_a
    if final_a is None:
        final_a = np.ones(256, dtype=np.float64)

    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 30.0:
        best_exact_score = np.inf
        best_exact_a = None
        seen = 0
        for a in candidates:
            if time.monotonic() > deadline - 5.0:
                break
            sc = get_exact_score(a)
            seen += 1
            if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_exact_score:
                best_exact_score = sc
                best_exact_a = a.copy()

        sc = get_exact_score(final_a)
        seen += 1
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_exact_score:
            best_exact_score = sc
            best_exact_a = final_a.copy()

        if best_exact_a is not None:
            final_a = best_exact_a
            print(f"best exact score after validation: {best_exact_score:.10f} candidates={seen}", flush=True)

    final_a = normalize_nonneg(final_a)
    if final_a is None:
        final_a = np.ones(256, dtype=np.float64)
    return [float(x) for x in final_a]
```
